#2851. Fernando Mendoza’s tests not getting any easier. Broncos will show how much rookie learned

Fernando Mendoza compared what Mike Macdonald did to him Sunday to playing chess.

He left out the part about the chessboard being set up inside an octagon.

The Seahawks' defensive wizard of a head coach challenged the Raiders rookie with shifting coverages and pressure packages, then let his defenders deliver the physical consequences. There were punches, a little taekwondo and enough swift kicks to leave Mendoza studying the board from the canvas.

Fernando Mendoza (15) of the Las Vegas Raiders warms up before a game at Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

Now comes Denver, where another accomplished defensive coach and a rested collection of pass rushers await his second NFL start.

Broncos defensive coordinator Vance Joseph has had the bye week to prepare for the Raiders. Macdonald just provided fresh film of what confused Mendoza, what slowed his decisions and what happened when an encouraging first half gave way to a miserable second.

Joseph will have his own ways of asking whether the rookie learned anything.

That is how this works. Put a weakness on film and the next opponent will make you prove you have addressed it. Fix that one and someone will start looking for another.

Mendoza understood as much after his debut, accepting responsibility for the sacks and acknowledging that Macdonald had fooled him. His willingness to own those mistakes was encouraging. Sunday offers the first opportunity to show what he has done about them.

Brock Bowers #89 and Fernando Mendoza #15 of the Las Vegas Raiders bump fists on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, at Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas. Getty Images

Mendoza completed 11 of 13 passes for 90 yards in the first half against Seattle, distributing the ball to eight receivers and scoring on a 10-yard quarterback draw. He looked comfortable enough to give the Raiders reason to believe they could win.

Then Seattle adjusted. Mendoza finished the second half 6-of-16 passing, and the Raiders struggled to sustain anything.

The challenge in Denver is recognizing those changes quickly enough to keep the offense moving. That means identifying pressure, trusting the appropriate answer and understanding when a play has reached its expiration date.

It also means getting considerably more help.

The Raiders rushed for 65 yards on 24 attempts against Seattle. Asking a rookie to keep solving difficult passing situations while the running game produces so little is an exhausting way to develop a quarterback.

Klint Kubiak has to find ways to create manageable downs, move Mendoza when the situation calls for it and give him throws he can make decisively. The offensive line must provide enough time for those decisions to matter.

Denver has won five straight games, and Nik Bonitto supplied a reminder of its ability to finish opponents with his late strip-sack against Carolina before the bye.

Mendoza is walking into another difficult classroom, with the crowd adding to the demands.

Someday, he might look back on these first two starts with a debt of gratitude. The lessons could help him recognize trouble sooner, play faster and become the quarterback the Raiders envisioned.

For now, he would probably appreciate learning a few of them without another black eye.

Miller has not been as sharp as the Raiders have come to expect, and this would be a good week to rediscover his best football.

Bonitto remains one of the league's most dangerous rushers, even with his sack production trailing the pace of his recent seasons. His strip-sack against Carolina showed how quickly he can decide a game, and the bye provided time to recharge for the stretch run.

Las Vegas Raiders players celebrate Fernando Mendoza's touchdown against the Seattle Seahawks in Las Vegas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Seattle won 21-13. Getty Images

Miller must handle Bonitto's speed without becoming vulnerable to his counters. Mendoza can help by getting the ball out on time and maintaining his position in the pocket.

But the Raiders need their veteran left tackle to win enough individual battles to give their rookie a chance.

Stokes needs a response game after an uneven season took a conspicuous turn against Seattle.

Kubiak briefly pulled him after plays on which the coach said his effort fell short. The message was clear. A player the Raiders trust to handle difficult coverage assignments still has to finish every down.

Waddle can make any lapse expensive. His speed puts pressure on a cornerback's positioning, and his ability to create separation demands concentration throughout the route.

Stokes must stay disciplined when Bo Nix extends plays and keep competing if the ball goes elsewhere. The Raiders' pass rush also has to finish its work.

Against Denver's most dangerous receiver, coverage and effort will both be under scrutiny.

Mendoza looks more comfortable in his second start, gets the ball out faster and throws his first NFL touchdown pass. There will be enough improvement to give the Raiders something tangible to build on. But their inability to establish a running game keeps putting too much on the rookie, and the defense gives Denver too many opportunities to answer. Coming off the bye, the Broncos have the preparation time and pass rush to force mistakes. Mendoza takes a step forward. The Raiders still come up short.

That many Seahawks defenders pressured Mendoza in his first start, illustrating how much the Raiders must improve their protection before facing Denver's varied rush.

#2849. Cathie Wood’s Ark Invest was a big buyer of Anthropic as she tells CNBC she’s ‘quite optimistic’

Cathie Wood was a major buyer of Anthropic following its debut Thursday on the Nasdaq, adding shares in all her major funds.

Wood's firm, which already owned private shares of the artificial intelligence company in its Ark Venture Fund leading up to the initial public offering, bought $90.8 million worth of Anthropic in six funds, according to a trade notification from the firm.

"We've owned Anthropic since early '23," Wood told CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Friday. "It's our best performing equity by far in ARKVX, our venture fund. ... Our confidence has grown with time as it really took over the enterprise" AI business.

Anthropic shares jumped 17% in their first day, but closed well off the highs of the session and were headed for sizable losses Friday. The Nasdaq, which was expected to get a boost from a major IPO reviving enthusiasm about the technology trade, fell by 2% on Thursday.

"We're climbing a wall of worry," said Wood. "Everybody is worried about AI hype and the political situation after the election, but I think we'll look back and say, OK this is more like the '80s and '90s, climbing a wall of worry you know with bricks much like what we have today: inflation, interest rates, wars, new technologies and so forth so quite optimistic here."

The Anthropic stakes are relatively small across the funds, but Wood typically will buy shares in companies she likes steadily during long stretches. Wood said she is "averaging down" with her purchases of Anthropic.

In her $6.1 billion Ark Innovation ETF, Anthropic is a 0.3% stake. The biggest holdings in that fund are Tesla and SpaceX, representing more than 21% of the fund together.

Ark Innovation, which retail investors piled into during the pandemic as it gained about 150% in 2020, tumbled the subsequent next two years, losing 67% in 2022 alone. Wood's following was dented after the big losses.

The fund had a comeback stretch as the bull market revved back up from 2023 to 2025, but has fallen on hard times again this year, down more than 10% in 2026. The S&P 500 is up 4.8% this year.

Wood dismissed comparisons of today's environment to the dot-com bubble.

"Back then if an Anthropic had gone public, it probably would have gone up threefold the first day," she said. "That's how crazy things were."

#2847. Magic star Franz Wagner talks finally playing against brother Moe in win over Nets

Magic star Franz Wagner talks finally playing against brother Moe in win over Nets appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Orlando Magic escaped the Brooklyn Nets with a 113-111 NBA Cup victory on Friday night, and for Franz Wagner, the night carried a little extra meaning. Not only did the Magic forward hit the game-winner, but he also faced off against his older brother, Moritz "Moe" Wagner, in an official NBA game for the very first time.

The two brothers spent five seasons together in Orlando before Moe signed a two-year, $19 million deal with the Nets in free agency this past summer. That meant Friday's matchup was the first time the Wagners ever shared the floor as opponents in a game that counted.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"In these games, obviously we play for a little bit of extra motivation," Franz said. "We obviously try to win every game, but I think there's a little bit of something different about the games."

#2845. Hawks keep NBA Cup chances alive, defeat Hornets 111-97

The Atlanta Hawks were in action on Friday evening to face the Charlotte Hornets in an NBA Cup game. The Hawks came into this game 1-1 in group play, while the Hornets are 0-2 and looking for their first win. This was a big game for both teams if they wanted to stay in the race.

The Hawks were still without Kingston Flemings for the third straight game, while the Hornets were without Kon Knueppel, Grayson Allen, Dennis Schroder and Grant Williams.

Jalen Johnson got it started for the Hawks, turning defense into offense for an easy two points.

Dyson Daniels was up next for the Hawks, and he knocked down a 3-pointer to keep things rolling.

It was a six-point lead for the Hawks, but the Hornets were able to erase that quickly, and also extended their own lead to as much as eight halfway through the quarter.

Johnson kept finding an open lane to the rim, and he did it off his fourth steal of the game.

The Hornets made their run down the stretch, but the Hawks continued to execute down the stretch. It helped them to ultimately win the game, and they had a solid point differential in the end to help them in the NBA Cup standings.

Johnson finished with 26 points, six rebounds and six assists, Wiggins finished with 19 points, Okongwi finished with 16 points and seven rebounds, and Daniels finished with 11 points, eight rebounds and 10 assists.

#2844. 49ers had enough to worry about — then Vikings got Kirk Cousins

The 49ers were already going to learn something about themselves Sunday night in Mexico City.

Then the Vikings went and got Kirk Cousins.

Minnesota Vikings quarterback Kirk Cousins (8) looks on during the fourth quarter against the Green Bay Packers in Green Bay, Wis., Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

What had the makings of a classic confrontation between San Francisco's offense and Minnesota's defense suddenly became something considerably more revealing. The Vikings have a quarterback their head coach trusts, throwing to receivers he knows, with a defense capable of carrying a team deep into January.

For the 49ers, that makes this a timely examination of where they stand among the NFC's best.

They will arrive in Mexico City with an offense averaging a league-leading 399.8 yards and 31.2 points per game, second in the NFL. Minnesota counters by allowing a league-low 14 points per game and 273.3 yards, second fewest.

The deeper numbers reinforce the matchup. The 49ers lead the league in offensive points per possession at three. The Vikings allow a league-low 1.24 points per possession.

Kyle Shanahan has the personnel and imagination to make even a good defense look uncomfortable. Brock Purdy can distribute the ball to multiple threats, and Christian McCaffrey's ability to move between the backfield and the passing game forces opponents to account for him wherever he lines up.

Brian Flores has spent the season making quarterbacks uncomfortable before they even take the snap. His fronts invite questions about who is coming, who is dropping and how much time the quarterback will have to sort through it.

Watching those two work against each other would have justified the trip by itself.

But Cousins' return changes the demands on San Francisco's defense, too.

Minnesota acquired him from the Raiders last week and immediately put him back in an offense he operated under Kevin O'Connell in 2022 and 2023. Five days after the trade, Cousins threw for 207 yards and two touchdowns in a 30-10 win at Green Bay.

Brock Purdy (13) of the San Francisco 49ers looks to pass against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas. Getty Images

There was an interception mixed in. There will be adjustments as he catches up with everything that has changed since he left.

Still, the Vikings suddenly look capable of making an opponent pay for devoting its entire week to figuring out their defense.

Cousins understands O'Connell's expectations. He knows how Justin Jefferson sees coverage and where Jordan Addison can hurt it. Those relationships give Minnesota a chance to accelerate a transition that normally requires considerably more time.

And they give the 49ers a second problem to solve.

San Francisco just scored 46 points against Dallas and needed nearly every one of them. Allowing 40 points in a victory leaves plenty to address, especially with O'Connell getting another week to prepare his reunited offense.

The 49ers can welcome that challenge. Their 8-1 record has earned them a place near the front of the conference. Beating a 7-2 Minnesota team with this defense and a familiar quarterback settling back in would provide another reason to believe they can stay there.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan, left, and general manager John Lynch watch players warm up on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. AP Photo/Julio Cortez

Sunday offers the kind of opponent that can expose a weakness while there is still time to fix it.

Or confirm that the 49ers have fewer of them than almost everyone else.

Two coaches operating at the top of their games will spend Sunday trying to take away what makes the other so effective.

Shanahan wants Purdy playing with clarity, using motion, formations and the threat of the run to create favorable throws. Flores wants that picture changing, preferably after Purdy has committed to what he thinks he sees.

McCaffrey could determine who gets his way. Sending him into a route gives Purdy another dangerous option. Keeping him in protection helps account for the pressure Flores brings.

Shanahan's challenge is preserving enough receiving options to punish Minnesota's aggression. For Flores, it is forcing quick decisions without making the answers obvious.

Every protection adjustment and late defensive movement will have a purpose.

O'Connell and Morris helped the Rams win a Super Bowl following the 2021 season, with O'Connell coordinating the offense and Morris running the defense. Those practice field confrontations now become part of the preparation for a game with considerable NFC implications.

O'Connell has his quarterback back, and with Cousins comes the freedom to call plays knowing how the man executing them will respond. That familiarity makes Minnesota feel more complete.

Minnesota Vikings head coach Kevin O'Connell looks on after a game against the Green Bay Packers at Lambeau Field in Green Bay, Wis., Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Morris understands how O'Connell builds an offense and how its concepts fit together. He can use that knowledge to challenge Cousins' first read and force him to hold the ball.

O'Connell will anticipate those adjustments, of course.

That is what makes this compelling. Two former colleagues know how much the other knows, and both must decide when to break their tendencies.

Something feels right about Cousins finding his way back to Minnesota after 2 ½ seasons away, with his recovery from the Achilles injury that ended his 2023 season part of that journey. He returns to a more complete team, with a defense capable of giving him a chance to finish what he started. The 49ers will make Minnesota earn every bit of this. But with another week alongside O'Connell, Cousins makes enough timely throws, and Flores finds one late stop to give the reunion another memorable chapter.

The Vikings sacked Jordan Love eight times in Cousins' return last Sunday. Purdy's protection will face a considerably tougher test than the reunion storyline alone suggests.

#2842. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

#2841. Fan dies after falling during a fight at 49ers-Cowboys game

A man who attended Sunday’s game between the 49ers and Cowboys has died due to injuries sustained in a fight during the game.

Via The Independent, 36-year-old Marco Narro was involved in an altercation with another fan near kickoff to the game. During the fight, Narro fell. After the incident, Narro stayed and watched the game.

During the game, he started to complain about pain. He and a friend sought attention from paramedics after the game. Narro was then transported to a local hospital.

On Wednesday, Narro died. An autopsy is pending.

The Arlington Police Department has opened a formal investigation. The prevalence of security cameras at AT&T Stadium should be useful when it comes to reconstructing the events.

The situation underscores one of the basic risks of engaging in fights at sporting events. It doesn’t take much to suffer, or to inflict, a very serious injury.

#2840. Reitz girls basketball wins season opener against North

It took some time before Reitz girls basketball began its season.

The Panthers have been battling it out with each other throughout the last month during practice. But then came November 20 — the season opener against North.

More: South Knox girls basketball out to prove it is one of the top teams in Indiana

Reitz would have the opportunity to showcase its extended preparation in front of its home crowd. On the other hand, the Huskies came into the night having already played three games.

The Panthers' lack of game action showed itself, but not enough to put the outcome in North's favor. After Reitz led by just one point (43-42) entering the fourth quarter, coach Brock Stone's group pulled away with a 25-14 effort in the final frame to win 68-56.

Panthers guard/forward Kierstyn Kaiser led the way with 27 points. The junior showcased an ability to finish inside and a willingness to make plays for others.

Freshman guard Molly Swedenburg was big down the stretch. Seven of her eight points came in the fourth quarter.

And while there were lapses in concentration, the Reitz defense created the separation. Several turnovers were created because of the Panthers' ball pressure putting North in tough positions to escape.

It was a win in the first game, but head coach Brock Stone certainly was not satisfied with the performance.

"I don't think we played up to the standard that we try to uphold," Stone said. "I was disappointed in how we guarded the ball and how we rebounded. In the past, that's (been) part of who we are."

Stone acknowledged the schedule playing a factor in how his team played. The rustiness was inevitable against an SIAC opponent that has been playing since the first week of November.

"I don't like the way our schedule is set up," Stone said. "When you haven't been able to play a game, but then you come out against a team that our girls have grown up with their whole lives, it's just something I've got to fix in the offseason this year to get us some games so that we're ready for those moments."

While it might have affected its readiness for the opener, long term, Stone is not worried about his team's ability to meet the standard. In the fourth quarter, what he hopes to see more of was on display.

Reitz created several multi-possession leads, but North always had a response. Well, up until the fourth quarter.

North is now 2-2 after the loss on November 20. First-year coach Brad Elliot felt his group was overwhelmed by Kaiser in the fourth quarter. The junior scored 14 of her 27 points in the final frame.

"Kaiser taking it to the basket really got us," Elliott said. "Her length got us there at the end."

Elliott felt his team did not make it tough enough for Kaiser on those drives into the paint. Rather than making her earn it at the line, there were a few soft fouls that resulted in and-one opportunities.

With Kaiser's size advantage, he would have liked for his team to anticipate her habits. Instead, they found it difficult to stay in front of the junior and secure defensive rebounds.

Still, Elliott was proud of North's resilience. Senior guard Madelynn Walters led the Huskies with 13 points.

North will be at Castle (0-5) on November 24. Reitz has a quick turnaround — playing the second half of a back-to-back on November 20 at home against New Albany (1-3).

#2838. Vance Joseph says Riley Moss has the ‘perfect personality’ to play opposite Pat Surtain II

Now that Riley Moss is locked down long-term he will be Robin to Pat Surtain being Batman for years to come. That he has been able to hold up opposite of the best cornerback in the NFL is a testament to him. It's not easy being the guy every offense looks to exploit because the guy on the other side is just too good.

Defensive coordinator Vance Joseph touched on the extension and why Moss was so deserving of it.

"I'll start with this, he's always been very athletic," Joseph said on Thursday. "He's competitive. To play opposite of Pat Surtain, that's a tough job. Outside of Bo Nix on gameday, it's probably Riley. So that part he showed from Day One. Obviously, year-by-year as he played, he's playing cleaner football. He's played well this year. He's been nicked a little bit as far as missing some games, but he's playing good ball."

He went on to dig into what makes Moss such a vital long-term piece for Denver's defense.

"No one understands his gameday because Pat's out there at left corner, and they never look at Pat," Joseph explained. "They do sometimes, but mostly they don't. They're trying to find Riley. So that's a tough job. He's the perfect personality for that job. Most corners wouldn't have the resolve to kind of over, and over again just take 12 targets a game, and two penalties here and there. He's competing. As a player, if something bad happens, he can come back in five seconds. I'm still shook by it, but Riley [says], 'Coach, I'm good.' I'm like, 'Really?' That's a superpower. He is resilient, and he's one of us."

Those words 'perfect personality' stuck out to me in every way. And like VJ said, none of us can understand what his gameday is like. To be targeted over and over again and have to battle through the mistakes that any human would make if given enough tries to make one. Just heading VJ say those things has actually made my appreciation for what Moss goes through week in and week out grow a bit more.

More importantly, he has cleaned up aspects of his game. He still has a few penalties against him, but most of them are garbage calls anyway. What Riley Moss does for this defense cannot be understated. I'm glad they've got him locked in long-term.

No Bull Perspective: The Importance of Riley Moss

I'm honestly surprised at how many comments I've seen since Riley Moss' extension that are casting shade on Moss, the extension, or Moss' play. I think the biggest obstacle to understanding this extension and player comes from how hard it is to understand the role of cornerbacks in the NFL. So let's start there.

Broncos vs Raiders 5 questions: How is Raider Nation feeling about Fernando Mendoza?

Fernando Mendoza made his first career NFL start for the Raiders. Now he gets a taste of the rivalry with the Broncos.

Why extending Riley Moss was a smart decision for the Denver Broncos

The Denver Broncos continued their tradition of rewarding their homegrown talent. Yesterday, news broke that the Broncos had agreed to terms with fourth-year cornerback Riley Moss on a four-year contract worth $67 million. As of now, the guarantees have not been made available. However, the average annual value makes him the NFL's 23rd highest paid corner. In my opinion, that's quite the bargain.

The Denver Broncos battle the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday. Here is a deeper look at their Week 11 opponent.

Defensive Coordinator Vance Joseph praises Riley Moss for his resilience and toughness

On the day Riley Moss was extended, Defensive Coordinator Vance Joseph had glowing praise for the fourth-year cornerback.

Broncos aim to hit their stride at just the right time

The Broncos look to carry momentum from the bye and look to build on their success as the season enters the stretch run.

On CB Riley Moss's contract extension and the significance of keeping him in Denver long-term

"It's special. I think it speaks to the [Walton-Penner Family] Ownership [Group], the GM [General Manager George Paton] and the Head Coach [Sean Payton]. That's the program. That's the plan here to draft, develop and retain. So it's working to obviously maintain and get better each year. That's the key to this league. Keep your own, and find them early and pay them."

On maintaining a dominant defense year-to-year

"It's a standard. It's a standard of how you work, and how you gameplan and how you play on gameday. Having great depth obviously. We've had some injuries, and guys stepped up and played well for us. It's tough in this league to play really, really good year-to-year because teams obviously change. Once they get you on tape for three years, they kind of adjust to you. So making the right changes and keeping the core guys intact is key. We've lost some guys, but we're finding new guys and that's key also. For us this year, it's been more about the explosive plays. That's disappointing for me right now, but we're going to fix that. I'm looking forward to us playing better football because it hasn't been to my standard yet or our standard yet."

On S Devon Key making big plays on both defense and special teams against Carolina

"The first thing I'll say is I don't know if I've been a part now, this is 18 years from the NFL… Very few times I've seen a guy get a game ball in two areas in one game. So No. 1, that's a huge accomplishment and kudos to him for that. The thing that I want to say about Devon, probably the thing that's most impressive for me is that no matter how big his role is on defense, he does not want a diminished role on special teams. He is adamant about that with me. I know my role stepped up here, but I'm still doing 'X', 'Y' and 'Z'. I'm still doing these jobs. We do a good job of keeping the rep count, making sure it doesn't get too high or out of control during the game. I have a ton of respect for him. I'm not surprised at his success. I just watch his work ethic, his preparation. So to get an opportunity to make a play, again, not surprised just because I know the player, the football I.Q. and the work ethic. So really, really happy for him. An incredible accomplishment to get a game ball. I think officially he got credited for two, we had him for three tackles on special teams and plus the interception. Just a really, really great job by him."

On what the Raiders special teams unit is doing well

"They've really done a nice job. They got obviously a whole new staff. [Special Teams Coordinator] Joe DeCamillis, who's a name that's recognized around here. Obviously he's been a long time NFL special teams coordinator. I have a ton of respect for Joe. I've known Joe for a long time. Ironically, he was actually at Texas with my son a few years ago. So I think it starts with coaching. When you watch them on film, a huge emphasis in the phases. Then obviously the players, [Raiders RB] Dylan Laube's having a phenomenal year as a kick returner, kind of leads the league. It's not just the 95-yard touchdown. I feel like every time we watch the plays of the week, 40-yard return, 50-yard return, he's got these chunk plays. Downhill runner, really tough, breaks tackles. So he's done a really good job there. [P] AJ Cole's been one of the best punters in our league for the last five years or so. Not only is he punting the ball well, they're covering well. So Joe's done a really phenomenal job. I think [Head Coach] Klint [Kubiak], obviously I was with Klint in New Orleans. I think Klint has given Joe time to prepare, giving them the right pieces. So I think it comes down to a combination, but they're doing a couple things really, really well. They do some unique things as well, so we have to be prepared. Some things we haven't seen yet this year. So this is going to be a huge challenge for us. So kudos to them. I think this will be one of our toughest tests yet this year in this phase."

On Seahawks WR Tory Horton's kickoff return play last week against Las Vegas and when they review situational plays like that

"First of all, phenomenal heads-up play by them. Great coaching by the Seattle staff. It's actually that particular situation we had just covered two weeks earlier. It wasn't the tip back, but it was the time scenario. Obviously they're trying to keep one of the timeouts, trying to keep their opposition from getting the timeout there. So if you guys remember back last year, there was a play in the Chicago game. I think it was Week 1, a very similar play where the ball came out of the end zone and it kind of stole a timeout. So we keep hitting those. Now that we're seeing the ball in play 80 percent of the time, all these things are coming up. There was also a great play in the Washington game. Was it [Commanders WR Luke] McCaffrey, he made a really good play on the sideline where he got himself out of bounds, touched the ball. Those are huge plays. They're chunk plays because now the ball is at the 40 [-yard line] or you burn a timeout. So yes, situation. Our big day for that is Saturday. We kind of take about a half hour or so on Saturdays and review all those situations. So we'll certainly show those couple plays, but just constantly going through the game management part of it. I had a similar play a few years ago on a long field goal. [Former Dolphins WR] Jarvis Landry in Miami saved the field goal from going out of bounds and got a return. Now he didn't score. He got about a 30, 40 yarder. So I've seen that happen before. Then [former NFL RB] Cordarrelle Patterson, he was kind of famous for trying to keep the ball in play. A lot of people didn't realize you could bat the ball back forward and kind of keep it alive from going out of bounds. So now phenomenal play by Horton, phenomenal play by Seattle. We have our situational day on Saturdays where we kind of review all the things that could come up maybe once a season or once every five seasons, if you will."

On finding more efficiency in the run game

"It's a combination of a couple things. I think we can hit them with the variables and run with more conviction. We can do a better job of handling the fronts that we have, and dominating the five- and six-man boxes, and getting downhill, and being satisfied with five-, six-yard gains and getting a little bit more explosive after that. We've done a good job this year of not running too many negative runs. We haven't had negative one, twos. I know there were a couple last week, but for the most part of the last nine games, there haven't been many of those. We've been on track, but we can definitely do a better job of getting to second-and-5 as opposed to second-and-8 and second-and-9 and be a in a better position on second down."

On the offense putting up more points and the next step

"I think it's a combination of a few things. Our defense is doing a good job getting us the ball in short-field positions and us capitalizing. I think we've done a good job. Other than last week, I think there's a three week stretch where we were close to, if not 100 percent in red zone. So dominating that and getting touchdowns as opposed to three points. Those two combinations have been really good. I think we're playing complementary right now. Again the schedule's going to get a little tougher here pretty soon, and we have to continue to get going."

On how they can improve on third-and-long

"First of all, first and second down. Easy answer. When we are in those situations, I think it's important to get better in a hurry. That can be protection, that can be route depth, that can be trusting your eyes in the pocket. I just named three things. It's a combination. It really is. I think we're getting the plays in good enough, and we're doing a good job identifying pressures. However just kind of being 11 [players] as one a little bit better on seven-plus [yards] is important because we haven't been good enough there."

"I'll start by saying I'm just very thankful. I'm very thankful to be in Denver. I'm thankful for [Owner] Carrie [Walton Penner], and for [Owner & CEO] Greg [Penner], for [General Manager] George [Paton] and [Head Coach] Sean [Payton] believing in me. What they're building is really special, and I wanted to be a part of it, and I want to be a part of it as long as I can. It's filled with great people here in the facility. That's the biggest thing. They're always going after good people, and it's easy to build a good team around really good people. I want to thank them. I want to thank my parents because I wouldn't be where I'm at without them. They believed even when it got dark and sometimes I wouldn't believe. There are a lot of people I want to thank. My teammates especially, I wouldn't be able to do my job without them. I'll open it up to questions."

On why signing a contract extension now was the right move

"The biggest thing for me is fit, the people around me. I let my agent handle that stuff, and what he thought is best fit and best for me. I trust him. He's amazing. I'm glad we were able to get this done, but the biggest thing is not being satisfied. I was just talking to 'Court' (WR Courtland Sutton) in the weight room. Just talking about that feeling of it's really cool to get this money and stuff, but there's also work to be done. How do you manage feeling happy for yourself, but also knowing you have to go out and play well against the Raiders on Sunday? It's the 24-hour rule just like after a win. I'm going to enjoy it, but there's still a lot of work to do."

On the mentality that has allowed him to embrace the challenge of playing opposite of CB Pat Surtain II

"I think the biggest thing is it's how I was raised with my parents. The biggest thing was they let me fail so I would be able to figure out how to become successful through that, through that adversity. It's the reason I am who I am today. It's a hard job, but it's a rewarding job and I love it."

On the support of the Walton-Penner Family Ownership Group

"It's crazy because this is all I've ever known. I hear stories from some of my friends who are on other teams, and they say their training room isn't even close to what they had in college. That's crazy because here it's, 'Is it going to help us win?' Then they're going to try to invest and help us with that. That's why we've been able to grow so fast as a team and as a club is [Owner & CEO] Greg [Penner] and [Owner] Carrie [Walton Penner] really caring about us as players and creating that culture, that family culture."

#2837. Prediction markets outlook for stocks in 2026 dims amid AI sell-off

As stocks continue their tech-driven sell-off, prediction markets traders are seeing a bleaker outlook for the remainder of the year.

On platform Kalshi, speculators place just 6% odds that the S&P 500 will cross 8,000 in 2026. On Oct. 13, those odds were as high as 72%.

Contracts ask traders if the S&P 500 will go above various levels in 2026, and they're resolved using data from S&P Dow Jones Indices.

On Friday, the Nasdaq Composite turned negative for the year as investors continued to dump tech stocks, primarily those with exposure to the artificial intelligence build-out. The Dow Jones Industrial Average and the S&P 500 fell into correction territory, too.

The S&P 500 has slashed its year-to-date gain to just over 2%.

The Stock Trader's Almanac in a Thursday note broadly agreed that the hopes for a December rally are fading, with the caveat that a "Santa rally" could still appear at the end of the year.

"The market is under pressure and more downside can't be ruled out," the note said.

The Stock Trader's Almanac sees 2026 following the year-end pattern of 2018, when the Federal Reserve was in a tightening cycle in a midterm year. The Fed is widely expected to hike rates at its December meeting.

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

#2834. Pistons vs. Raptors final score: Cade vs. Scottie battle goes to Raps

Two former teammates and two Eastern Conference teams traded body blows all night. A Cade Cunningham 3-pointer to put the Pistons up 102-101 with 20 seconds left was answered by a Scottie Barnes driving layup over two defenders to give the Raptors the lead they would not relinquish. When a Kevin Huerter three came up short as the buzzer sounded, it was decided. Raptors 105-102.

Cunningham led Detroit with 23 points and nine assists, but he was outshined on this night by his former Montverde teammate Barnes, who led all scorers with 29 points and hit his first nine shots of the night.

While it was appointment television watching the two top players on each team matched up with each other, the rest of the game was pretty much a horror show for the Pistons. Distilling why the Pistons dropped this game will sound very familiar to Pistons fans — not owning the paint.

Detroit surrendered 54 points inside to Toronto and gave up crucial offensive rebounds late. It's one thing to see Barnes put a great game on you, but Detroit was owned inside by rookie Allen Graves making his first start of the season. He scored 20 points, had 10 rebounds, including four on the offensive end. Detroit simply had no answer for him. Certainly not John Collins.

The overmatched emergency starting center struggled again in just under 20 minutes of action. He was just 1-of-4 from the floor for three points and six rebounds. The return of Isaiah Joe from injury isn't looking very different from when he was playing early in the season — he's still struggling to find his shot. Joe was 1-of-6 from deep tonight. Getting those kinds of performances from your two highest-profile offseason acquisitions is not the recipe for winning basketball.

The other major issue tonight is that you were facing one of the worst-shooting offenses in the NBA and on this night, they could not miss. Some of that had to do with Detroit's occasionally passive defense. But tonight, the Raptors players were just collectively on one. We already covered how hot Barnes was, especially early, but the sweet shooting was up and down the roster.

The rookie Graves was 6-of-9, Jakob Poeltl was 5-of-7, and Immanuel Quickly was 6-of-10. Collectively, Toronto shot 67% inside the arc tonight. Again, I'm going to point to the big black hole in the middle of Detroit's lineup. A combination of John Collins and Paul Reed as your only big men is just not going to get it done in the NBA. The Pistons need Jalen Duren to come back desperately.

The loss to the Raptors drops the Pistons to 1-1 in NBA Cup play, but then again, I refuse to care about the NBA Cup. Detroit now goes on a mini road trip to Atlanta on Sunday and Orlando on Tuesday before enjoying seven of the next eight games at home.

(8-2) defeated CREC, 41-6 at New Britain, to earn a spot in Class S.

The CIAC playoff qualifier projections are determined using the CIAC playoff formula, using OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna medium model and verified by GametimeCT using the current CIAC football playoff standings and formula.

All qualifiers remain unofficial until the CIAC's football committee meets the day after the regular season concludes on Thanksgiving morning. The playoffs begin Tuesday, Dec. 1 at the sites of the top-four seeds in each of the six divisions.

#2832. Raiders have 7 weeks to build foundation for Fernando Mendoza

If the Raiders wanted to ease Fernando Mendoza into his new job, they have a peculiar way of showing it.

First came the Seahawks, who turned his starting debut into a chess match with consequences. Six times, those consequences involved Mendoza being dropped to the ground with the ball still in his hands.

Next up are the Broncos, rested from their bye and waiting in Denver. After that, the Browns in Cleveland.

It is difficult to imagine a much more treacherous introduction to professional quarterbacking. Three defenses capable of making experienced quarterbacks uncomfortable, with the last two getting the rookie in their own buildings.

Fernando Mendoza #15 and Cody White #82 of the Las Vegas Raiders warm up at Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. The Seahawks won 21-13. Getty Images

The schedule will eventually offer something more forgiving. For now, Mendoza is getting an education that requires an ice bath afterward.

And somewhere down the road, he might appreciate it.

The disguised coverages, the pressure arriving from unexpected places, the difference between what a defense shows before the snap and what it becomes afterward. Every uncomfortable experience gives him something to recognize the next time he sees it.

But there is another education taking place, and the Raiders need to pay just as much attention to it.

John Spytek, Tom Brady and Klint Kubiak are learning what they have to put around their young quarterback.

That makes Sunday's game against Denver, and the six remaining afterward, an evaluation of everyone involved in making this work.

Mendoza has responsibilities. He has to process information faster, understand when to abandon a play and get the ball out before a manageable situation becomes a drive-killing sack.

He accepted those responsibilities after the Seattle game. Good for him.

The entire building needs to do the same.

The Raiders rushed for 65 yards on 24 attempts against the Seahawks. Brock Bowers hurt his ankle on the opening series, and an offense already short on dependable answers had even fewer.

Las Vegas Raiders General Manager John Spytek arrives at Allegiant Stadium for a game against the Seattle Seahawks in Las Vegas on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

Those are organizational problems with consequences for the quarterback.

Start with the interior offensive line. Mendoza needs room to step into throws and a running game that makes second down feel like an opportunity. The Raiders have to determine which players can provide that consistently and where upgrades are necessary.

Every failed block deserves examination, including whether the problem was talent, technique or communication. Those distinctions will matter when Spytek starts deciding how to spend another offseason.

Then there is the receiver the Raiders still need.

A premier talent alongside Bowers would change how defenses prepare for Las Vegas. Give Mendoza someone who threatens downfield, commands attention and wins when the coverage is good, and the entire offense benefits.

Bowers gets more favorable opportunities. The running game faces different defensive decisions. Mendoza gets a receiver he can trust when the play requires someone to make something happen.

The Raiders need that kind of two-headed problem for opponents.

They also need to understand that supporting a quarterback extends to the possessions when he is standing on the sideline.

A defense that cannot consistently stop the run gives the opposing offense control of the game. It keeps Mendoza waiting, limits his opportunities and increases the pressure to produce when he finally gets the ball back.

The interior defensive line needs more star power and ruggedness. Someone has to disrupt the blocking, hold the point of attack and make an offense reconsider running directly at the Raiders.

Maxx Crosby needs a reliable complementary pass rusher, too. The attention he commands should create opportunities that another defender regularly turns into sacks, hurried throws and stalled drives.

The secondary has promising young players whose development deserves investment and patience. But young defensive backs also benefit when the quarterback has to throw on schedule and the defense can anticipate passing situations.

Fox Sports broadcast analyst Tom Brady looks on before a game between the Dallas Cowboys and San Francisco 49ers in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Better run defense creates longer third downs. Better pressure helps coverage. More stops give the offense additional chances and a little breathing room.

These next games will help reveal which pieces belong and which must be added.

The Raiders selected Mendoza because they believe he can become a great quarterback. His first starts offer the beginnings of that picture, with every adjustment and mistake adding detail.

They also expose the work required around him.

Sunday is another opportunity for Mendoza to show what he learned.

It is another opportunity for the Raiders to figure out what they must do about what they are learning.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2829. Nets shake up everything but the outcome in heartbreaking fourth straight loss

Team meetings. Lineup changes. Whatever it took. Brooklyn tried everything to shake up a three-game losing skid.

The Nets finally showed some heart Friday night. But then they got it broken in a 113-111 loss to the Magic in front of a crowd of 17,067 at Barclays Center.

Franz Wagner hit a go-ahead 14-footer with 2.8 seconds left, and Julius Randle missed a desperation 3-pointer at the buzzer on the other end.

Brooklyn Nets forward Danny Wolf shoots as Orlando Magic center Wendell Carter Jr. defends on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y. Robert Sabo for NY Post

Randle finished with a team-high 27 points, and Brooklyn fought. They just couldn't quite close.

After the Nets (3-12) embarrassed themselves in Wednesday's 137-107 non-effort against Chicago, they held a team meeting, with some real talk about what went wrong and how to fix it.

Friday's effort showed it wasn't all just talk.

"No one wants to lose like we did [Wednesday] … so a lot needed to be said. A lot of guys have been doing a lot of talking; we've all been doing a lot of talking," Michael Porter Jr. said. "We've gotta string some games together. We've had spurts of good responses after really bad games, but that has to be a consistent theme. We can't just let that be responses to really bad losses."

Wednesday qualified as a really bad loss, the worst of a disappointing season.

Brooklyn Nets forward Julius Randle (30) goes up for a shot against the Orlando Magic at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y., Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

After that, the Nets had a look-in-the-mirror meeting that sources said included not only players, but also coaches.

"Everybody collectively. It was a good conversation, and everybody pitched into what they saw," Porter said. "There was a lot of internal discussion that I'll keep between the team. But just realizing we got to be better and addressing what needed to be addressed."

Orlando Magic forward Franz Wagner dunks during the second quarter against the Brooklyn Nets on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y. Robert Sabo for NY Post

Egor Dëmin — last year's lottery pick — was pulled out of the starting lineup. He was brought off the bench for the first time since Nov. 5, 2025, his rookie season.

Porter, who admitted not having the right energy in his five-point, 1-of-7 effort Wednesday, bounced back well. His layup off a Day'Ron Sharpe feed gave the Nets a 63-62 edge coming out of the half.

The Nets coughed up 10 unanswered Orlando points, the run capped by Wagner (game-high 35 points).

Brooklyn Nets center Moritz Wagner defends against Orlando Magic forward Franz Wagner during a game Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y. Robert Sabo for NY Post

The Nets trailed 72-63, and things got worse when starting point guard Nolan Traore limped off with eight minutes left in the third with a hamstring injury. Those are tricky for any player, especially for a twitchy speedster such as Traore.

Brooklyn trailed 89-85 after Wagner found Desmond Bane for a 3-pointer with 1:24 left in the third.

But Wolf drilled a 3-pointer off a Randle feed to cap a 13-4 run and put the Nets ahead 98-93 with 8:36 left to play.

After Brown missed a fadeaway 19-footer with 9 seconds left, Wagner didn't miss the go-ahead bucket on the other end.

#2826. No. 8 Pomperaug football rallies past Notre Dame Prep in OT behind Jack Guinipero

FAIRFIELD - The Pomperaug football team has been stomping all over opponents with a relentless rushing attack this season.

But senior Jack Guinipero made one thing clear after the team's latest game.

"We can pass, too," he said with a laugh.

Guinipero threw a game-tying touchdown pass to Caleb McKee with 51 seconds remaining, then ran for an 8-yard score in overtime to give No. 8-ranked Pomperaug a dramatic 27-20 victory over Notre Dame Prep on Friday at Sacred Heart University's Campus Field.

Guinipero even had a hand, literally, on the final play when he knocked away a fourth-down pass from Notre Dame Prep's Jordan Razor.

But the tying pass, on a play Guinipero called "28 Rocket Pass," was the biggest highlight, with Guinipero firing a pass from the sidelines into the end zone, and McKee going up under double coverage to make the catch.

"That's something we've had on the back burner forever," Guinipero said. "It's one of those plays where, we've got it in our back pocket any time we need it. I hit my favorite receiver, Caleb McKee, an absolute dog and he went over two people for it. Beast, I love to see it."

While the pass was the highlight, the running game did the heavy lifting as usual, with Guinipero rushing for 119 yards and a touchdown, Dom Creaco rushing for 80 yards and two touchdowns, and Josh Coughlin rushing for 97 yards.

But the Panthers had to overcome three turnovers: two fumbles and an interception by Notre Dame Prep's Razor.

"What I loved about this was we overcame that," Pomperaug coach Tom Pereira said. "We kept fighting and fighting, held them out for a two (point attempt) to keep the game within reach, and then we made plays down the stretch. I challenged our defense before this game. Aside from that series coming out of the halftime, we really bottled up their running game and forced them to have to go vertically down the field."

The game was the regular-season finale for both teams, which had already unofficially secured playoff berths.

Pomperaug (9-1) will be making its first trip to the state tournament since finishing runner-up in the Class L championship in 2009. The Panthers are currently second in Class MM and will likely host a quarterfinal game.

Notre Dame Prep (8-2), which is in Class M, is back in the playoffs after missing the cut last season, but Friday's loss may have cost it a shot at a home playoff game. The Lancers are now sixth in Class M.

"Going into the playoffs, this is the type of game you want," Notre Dame Prep coach John Johnson said. "This was the most physical team we've played all year long and our guys fought the entire game. We got put in situations where you're thinking, do we go for two? Do we kick it? Do we go to overtime? All those things that can pop up in the playoffs when it's do or die time popped up tonight so this is a really good learning tool."

Pomperaug was chasing Notre Dame Prep for much of the second half.

Ja'cari Harris scored on a 24-yard run in the third quarter to put the Lancers ahead, and quarterback JJ Studeny connected with Marvel Johnson on the conversion pass for a 14-7 lead.

The Panthers answered with an 80-yard drive that ended with a 2-yard touchdown run by Creaco, his second of the game. Notre Dame stuffed the conversion run, keeping its one-point lead at 14-13 with 9:07 remaining.

Harris, who had 111 rushing yards for the Lancers, opened the next possession with a 33-yard run and set up an eventual 10-yard touchdown by Studeny for a 20-13 lead with 6:57 on the clock. Notre Dame Prep went for two to try to make it a nine-point game, but the pass failed.

That led to another long drive by the Panthers and Guinipero's touchdown pass to McKee in the final minute.

Guinipero then scored on an 8-yard run in overtime, and Pomperaug shut down the Lancers for the win.

Jack Guinipero, Pomperaug: The senior was everywhere. He threw the game-tying touchdown pass, rushed for the game-winning touchdown, recovered a fumble and had 119 rushing yards overall.

"It all started back, even three years ago, Thanksgiving, right after Notre Dame Prep my sophomore year. We started up that real winter lifting program. We lifted 24-7, 365 (days a year), and we're conditioned for situations like this. We can dig deep and come out on top no matter what."- Pomperaug's Jack Guinipero

#2823. Saints officially waive S Justin Reid

Safety Justin Reid already penned his good-bye missive to the Saints, but his departure did not become official until 4 p.m. ET.

The Saints waived Reid on Friday, with the move made official on the NFL’s personnel notice.

Reid asked for his release this week, and the Saints have granted it.

“In life and sports, things don’t always go the way you planned or hoped they would,” Reid wrote on social media. “Unfortunately, this is one of those moments. Reid joined the Saints as a free agent in 2025, and he started all 24 games he played for the team. He had 118 tackles, an interception and a half-sack.

#2819. NFL will consider requiring minimum-night stays for international games

After the 49ers and the Rams took dramatically different approaches to their arrival in Melbourne for the first-ever Australia game, we raised the question of whether the league should require teams to get there early enough to advance the goal of promoting American football to international audiences.

Via The Athletic, the NFL will consider adopting a minimum-night stay for teams playing internationally.

“Currently, we leave those football decisions on when the team arrives in the market to the club,” NFL executive V.P. of club business, major events, and international Peter O’Reilly told The Athletic. “It is something that we’ll consider moving forward on: Do we put some minimum nights in a market in there? It’s something we’ll look at.”

The league already does it for the Super Bowl, mandating a full-week experience that includes multiple press conferences, the annual “Opening Night” media extravaganza, and practicing at designated facilities.

“Having access to players and that type of experience when they’re in the market does have a lasting impact,” O’Reilly said. “That’s a factor when you start to think about encouraging or potentially looking at a policy where you’d want a club in the market for more than the day before.”

Beyond impacting strategic considerations, it would add to the total expense. The money would be worth it, if it further enhances the league’s overall reason for going global.

#2815. 2026 Week 11 rooting guide for Cowboys fans

Outside of Dallas dropping their game to the 49ers, a lot went right for the Cowboys in Week 10. We need that to keep going for the rest of the season, both for the NFC East contention and our admittedly razor-thin hopes of a Plan B in the wild card race.

A Cowboys upset over San Francisco sure would've been nice given how other games unfolded. The Giants getting upset by the Commanders and Green Bay getting crushed at home by Minnesota were not expected. These results helped keep a division title and the seventh seed in range, one of which is required for Dallas to still get into the playoffs.

Granted, that NFC East crown is far more reachable than a wild card spot. But a lot can happen over the next eight weeks, especially as some of these NFC North and West teams start beating each other up in divisional games. A lane could still open for the Cowboys, even if we're not good enough to use it.

1. San Francisco 49ers 8-1 (5-1 vs NFC)2. Detroit Lions 7-2 (4-1)3. New York Giants 5-3-1 (3-3-1)4. Carolina Panthers 4-5 (4-3)5. Minnesota Vikings 7-2 (5-1)6. Chicago Bears 7-2 (5-2)7. Green Bay Packers 6-4 (4-4)_______________________________8. Seattle Seahawks 6-4 (2-3)9. Los Angeles Rams 6-4 (4-2)10. Dallas Cowboys 4-6 (2-5)

The next two weeks are critical for Dallas, getting two gifts from the scheduling gods in the Titans and Eagles. At a combined 3-15, they're arguably the worst teams in each conference. If the Cowboys don't come out of this stretch at 6-6, then talking about anything but the next offseason becomes quite silly.

Aside from avoiding supreme embarrassment against Tennessee, what else do we need for the Cowboys from Week 11?

Obviously, we need another loss for the Giants. And while we appreciate Washington helping us out there last week, we don't need them going on some late run and becoming another problem in the division.

Philadelphia is different. They seem truly done for this season, so now it's more about not wanting them to get a top-tier draft pick. And this week, it'd be especially nice if they could upset Pittsburgh, who owes Dallas their fourth-round pick next April.

It'd be great for Dallas if one team was clearly running away with the NFC North, but this feels like it's going to be a tight battle through Week 18. As such, we need as many losses as possible for those teams so they stop monopolizing the wild card spots.

Green Bay is on their bye week, so no help there. But as long as this thing stays tight, there's a good chance that someone falls out of the playoff scene.

Of course, for Dallas to benefit, they'll also need to get past the Rams and Seahawks. Both of them are also on a bye, so we'll see if the Cowboys can inch closer this week and then take a fresh look at playoff implications for Thanksgiving weekend.

If the Cowboys do win the NFC East, it'd be nice to be the third seed. We don't really care who wins the NFC South, so long as they have a worse record than us. Carolina is the current leader, so that's an easy pick.

The Arizona-KC game is mostly meaningless. The Cardinals' head-to-head win over Dallas shouldn't matter by Week 18. At this point, more teams filling up the bottom of the standings, and potentially keeping the Eagles or Commanders away from the top picks, is something to root for.

#2811. NBA Cup 2026: James Harden puts up 51 in Cavs' win over Pacers; shorthanded Raptors fend off Pistons, end 6-game skid

James Harden played by far his best game in a Cleveland Cavaliers uniform on Friday night during NBA Cup group play.

The 37-year-old guard erupted for 51 points in a 128-119 home win over the Indiana Pacers, a season high and his most since joining the Cavaliers amid the 2025-26 campaign.

Cleveland (9-8) improved to 2-1 in Cup competition, knocking on the Miami Heat's door atop East Group B. Indiana (5-11), which trailed the Cavaliers by as many as 24 points, dropped to 1-2 in this year's NBA Cup.

Harden shot 15 of 26 from the floor, including 7 of 11 from beyond the arc. He also finished 14 of 14 from the charity stripe and with six rebounds and six assists. The one-time NBA MVP and 11-time All-Star now has the third-most 50-plus-point games in NBA history. With 26 such performances, he passed the late Kobe Bryant (25) on Friday and now trails just Michael Jordan (31) and Wilt Chamberlain (118).

While collecting his first 50-piece with the Cavaliers, Harden moved into ninth all-time for most combined NBA regular and postseason points. His handles were on full display. He rained down 3s, and he made an impact at the rim.

Toronto came into Friday missing a trio of starters and on a six-game losing streak. Darko Rajaković's team was playing without RJ Barrett, Collin Murray-Boyles and, of course, Kawhi Leonard, who's still working his way back from a knee injury.

And yet, the shorthanded Raptors (6-10) found a way on the road versus the Detroit Pistons in NBA Cup action, ending their skid with a riveting 105-102 win.

It's important to note that Detroit (10-5) isn't at full strength, either. Jalen Duren remains sidelined with a hamstring issue. Plus, Duncan Robinson is out with a quad strain.

The absence of some star power didn't diminish what turned out to be an incredible matchup between two squads in East Group A and between a pair of former Montverde Academy teammates. Toronto's Scottie Barnes and Detroit's Cade Cunningham went toe-to-toe, particularly down the stretch.

Cunningham, who clocked out with 23 points and nine assists, hit a go-ahead 3-pointer over Barnes with less than 20 seconds remaining. Then, with the Raptors down one point, Barnes drove on Cunningham and reclaimed the lead.

Cunningham tried to answer back but ended up with nowhere to go with the ball after picking up his dribble en route to the basket. He passed it, and Toronto rookie Allen Graves nabbed a steal that helped his group wrap up its victory.

Barnes had 29 points, 5 rebounds and 5 assists. Graves, a first-round pick from Santa Clara making his first career start, piled up 20 points, 10 rebounds and 3 steals.

After that nail-biter, Toronto is 2-1 in East Group A, whereas Detroit is 1-1. The bottom dweller of the group, the Milwaukee Bucks (0-2 in NBA Cup play), was eliminated from knockout round contention because the Raptors and Orlando Magic both won Friday.

The Magic left Brooklyn victorious 113-111, thanks to Franz Wagner, who scored 35 points on 13-of-25 shooting and knocked down a game-winning jumper with 2.4 ticks left against the Nets.

Orlando is 3-0 in NBA Cup games this year.

#2809. Colts officially place Daniel Jones on waivers

Daniel Jones is officially on the waiver wire.

The NFL transaction report has gone out this afternoon, and Jones appears as a waiver from the Colts. After the trade deadline, every player who gets released by his team has to go on waivers before he becomes a free agent.

That means any team that wants Jones on his current contract can claim him, but that is extraordinarily unlikely to happen. As the waiver transaction notes, Jones is a vested veteran with a fully guaranteed salary for the rest of this season. No team is going to want to claim that salary.

Once he clears waivers, however, he’ll be an unrestricted free agent, free to sign with any team, and it won’t be surprising if some team wants to add him for veteran depth. Jones has been terrible this season, but he’s been a competent starter at times in his career, and a team that wants an experienced backup may be interested in signing him for a minimal salary.

Jones was also waived almost exactly two years ago, on November 22, 2024. He cleared waivers and signed with the Vikings’ practice squad before signing with the Colts after that season.

#2808. Epic Early Season Matchup as Hurricanes host Edmonton: Preview, How to Watch, Game Thread

After only playing two hockey games over the span of eleven days thanks to the trip to Finland, the Carolina Hurricanes now sprint into their second game in three days. It's also the first of a back-to-back to make it three in four. Fortunately for Carolina they only have to face one of two teams ahead of them in points—though not points percentage—and home of the top two scoring forwards in the league in the Edmonton Oilers.

You'll remember that we are already in rematch territory as the first matchup happened almost exactly a month ago in Edmonton. That game was a 6-5 shootout loss for the Canes where both teams surrendered five goals in regulation but the goalies managed to draw back in the shootout, with Brandon Bussi only surrendering the one that won the game.

Since that game Edmonton has been chugging right along, using the games in hand they built up with Carolina's Finland trip to overtake the Canes and stand second overall in the league on points—with Minnesota standing at the top this second. Carolina, though, leads on points percentage, so depending on your metric this is either a battle of two versus three or one versus three. Either way for a game this close to American Thanksgiving, it's got some heft.

The Oilers are finishing a six game road trip that hasn't really given them any breaks, as this game is actually the first of a road back-to-back for them with game two happening in St. Louis. The road trip started with a back-to-back as well, and in between they played the two Florida teams. They, like Carolina, are coming off a loss Thursday but unlike Carolina it was in regulation to the Atlantic leaders Tampa Bay—a team Carolina will see Wednesday.

You could do a deep dive on the Oilers in terms of trying to get to the bones of what makes them tick, but really you'd be trying too hard. The only stat you need to see is the top point producers in the league and you'll not only find three Oilers in the top four, you'll find Connor McDavid just blasting the competition with 55 points. He's doing it with—get ready—42 assists. He's "only" got thirteen goals, but the way he distributes the puck out on the ice has made him arguably an even bigger threat than he normally would be. He's well ahead of the pace he set last year of 90 assists, but actually behind the goal scoring pace which would frighten the league.

As for someone who's ahead of his goalscoring pace from last year, let's remind you of his teammate and usual linemate Leon Draisaitl. He's leading the league in goals with 24 right now, and he's basically pacing his career high in 2021-22 where he scored 55. Most teams would be lucky to have either, and you know that as much as Rod Brind'Amour loves this team he'd also be thrilled to have at least one.

So for all the controversy and jokes that came with the Mike Babcock hire, right now if you're Edmonton you're feeling pretty good about where things stand. They are already six points ahead of Vegas for first in the division, and fourteen ahead of Anaheim for third. Even the goaltending has been steadier than expected with both Devon Levi and Tristan Jarry holding sub-2.50 GAAs and over .900 save percentages. It's taken stress off the idea of getting Frederik Andersen back to the crease, as it looks like he won't be playing until Christmas. That means it is extremely unlikely he'll be in town for what would have been an emotional welcome back video, which is a shame as this is Edmonton's only regular season visit to Raleigh. Look for him to be acknowledged in some way though, and at least he got to spend time with Mike Maniscalco and Tripp Tracy back in October when the Canes visited Edmonton.

Speaking of that last visit, Babcock apparently is choosing the balanced lines approach, sliding Draisaitl to center his own line with Vasily Podkolzin (12 G, 8 A, +17) and Mathieu Jospeh (3 G, 5 A, -4). That creates the other line of McDavid, Filip Hallander (0G, 0A, -2) and Kasperi Kapanen (6 G, 7 A, -5). You may notice no Ryan Nugent-Hopkins on either line, and that's because he's once again dealing with injuries. He wasn't on the ice for Friday's practice, and while Babcock left it open that he would play Saturday, signs are pointing to no. Meanwhile, it appears Carolina will be facing Jarry based on how Edmonton practiced on Friday, which would be a switch up since it was Levi who backstopped the Oilers last month.

So why would Babcock opt for the balanced line appraoch? Well, last game the four Centers for Carolina were Sebastian Aho, Logan Stankoven, Jesperi Kotkaniemi, and Skyler Brind'Amour. Now you'll take out Kotkaniemi and Brind'Amour, and put in Jordan Staal and Mark Jankowski. That significantly upgrades the defensive ability of the team, thus spreading the goal scoring out appears to be how Babcock is answering.

For the Canes one would think the lack of a Friday practice means that Rod Brind'Amour will leave things alone, but in his mind, he could also feel that moving Seth Jarvis back up to the Aho line and William Carrier back to the Staal line wouldn't need any practice as both are very familiar with those roles. It's also hard to argue about the production the four lines as constructed are having right now, and Jarvis has proven over and over that he can defend with the Staal line. You also wonder where Kotkaniemi and Jussi Valimaki figure into the lineup over the next two days, as the back-to-back could be a way to get these two productive players back onto the ice and giving two others a load management day.

The question is which goalie gets the nod, understanding that the other will play in Madison Square Garden tomorrow. Logic would seem to dictate that with the tighter turnaround the Canes will go with Bussi today and Pyotr Kochetkov in New York, but Brind'Amour may flip that around to see how the Russian does with a tight turnaround and acknowledge he wasn't the reason the Canes lost Thursday. We don't know for sure because today was just an optional skate for the team, and only about half of the rostered players took part but with both goalies.

One other thing to note—last Thursday Taylor Hall played in his 1,000th NHL game being the first to do so playing in a European game. As is custom, the Hurricanes have a celebration planned that will be held Saturday before faceoff. If it follows the celebration for Frederik Andersen—hey maybe that's where he makes a surprise appearance—there'll be a montage, a few gifts including a silver hockey stick, and a surprise or two. So if you're planning to go account for that ceremony, and if you're watching on TV be ready.

All of that PLUS the second appearance of the Hometown Remix jerseys mean the start of an absolutely loaded weekend for the Hurricanes. If you aren't going to be there in person, here's how you can catch the action:

TV: Hurricanes Hockey Network and NHL Network. Hanna Yates and Shane Willis will handle the pregame duties at 12:30, and then Mike Maniscalco handles play-by-play with Tripp Tracy providing color—You can catch HHN on Spectrum, DirectTV, and Fubo. Note this game will also be nationally broadcast on NHL Network for fans outside of the Hurricanes' home market.

Streaming Video: Hurricanes Hockey Network on Prime Video in the Hurricanes Footprint. Outside the footprint with the game on NHL Network, you will NOT be able to watch the game on ESPN+.

Radio: The pregame Storm Watch with Adam Gold on 99.9 The Fan starts at 12:30 PM, At 1 PM radio (consisting of 99.9, 730 The Game in Charlotte, ESPN New Bern 107.5/1490, and ESPN Greenville 107.5/1570) will pick up the Mike Maniscalco and Tripp Tracy TV simulcast.

Line: (Per DraftKings) Hurricanes -180, -1.5 (+136) /Kraken +150, +1.5 (-162). O/U 6.5 +110/-130

#2807. John Harbaugh endorses Cam Skattebo’s new anti-backflip stance

John Harbaugh is celebrating Cam Skattebo's decision to change how he reacts to scoring a touchdown.

New York Giants head coach John Harbaugh speaks at a press conference in East Rutherford, N.J., Wednesday, Nov. 18, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

New York Giants running back Cam Skattebo flips after a touchdown against the Washington Commanders in East Rutherford, N.J., Thursday, Nov. 12, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

#2800. Hopewell football wins instant classic against Propel in WPIAL semifinals

DORMONT — When the game is on the line and you need a play to win the game, you're going to call on your best player.

That's exactly the situation that Hopewell found itself in the WPIAL 3A high school football semifinal game against Propel Braddock Hills. After a stop on defense, the team called on junior quarterback and Penn State commit James Armstrong, who took a keeper to the end zone to send the team to the WPIAL championship with a 66-60 double-overtime win on Friday, Nov. 20.

"In that situation, nobody is getting the ball other than No. 5 [James Armstrong]," Hopewell interim head coach John Rosa said. "Everyone played great tonight and when you have a Division I quarterback, you put the ball in his hands and let him win you the game."

The semifinal victory for Hopewell will go down as one of the best games in WPIAL playoff history, featuring 126 combined points between the two sides.

But it wasn't a back-and-forth affair throughout, with Hopewell (12-1) having to claw its way back from multiple deficits, including a 16-point hole halfway through the third quarter.

After Propel Braddock Hills (8-4) struck for an 11-yard touchdown run by running back Daniel Stephens, Armstrong threw an interception to Mikal Hall, setting up an 85-yard pick-six that — in many games — would have been the nail in the coffin.

Following the pick-six, Hopewell answered, with Brody Rock and Avery Bokor combining on a kickoff return, as Rock flipped the ball to Bokor at the Propel 12-yard-line and ran the rest of the way.

Related Reading: Hopewell football head coach Matt Mottes suspended for WPIAL 3A semifinals

Required Reading: WPIAL high school football Beaver County semifinal scoreboard

Required Reading: WPIAL high school football semifinal Beaver County storylines

After the return, Hopewell recovered an onside kick and, a few plays later, Armstrong ran for his third score of the night. The junior finished with six total touchdowns and an even 200 yards rushing.

"Not saying that tonight was going to be my best game ever, but I told the guys that I was going to try my hardest," Armstrong said. "When I threw that pick-six, I was thinking to myself that it was going to be difficult to come back from. But once I got to the sideline, I knew I just needed to keep working hard because I knew the defense would have my back."

That Hopewell defense came up big when it mattered most, despite what the box score said.

Just like he has all season, Propel athlete and Colorado commit Gabe Jenkins has terrorized opposing defenses and against Hopewell it was no different, finishing with 357 yards and five total touchdowns.

However, that Hopewell defense intercepted Jenkins twice, including once on the goal line with Propel threatening to score in the second quarter and also breaking up a pass in the end zone during the second overtime period.

"Our kids bent and had a lot of adversity throughout this game," Rosa said. "When this game started, we faced a lot of adversity, but all our coaches kept telling them that we had a lot of game left. They could have given up, but they didn't. This group kept working and I am so proud of them."

Hopewell will now shift its focus to the WPIAL Class 3A championship game, where it is making its first appearance since 2009.

The matchup will feature the much-anticipated Vikings and top-seeded Avonworth, a team that Hopewell beat back in Week 1.

"I've been going down to Acrisure since I was a kid to watch these WPIAL championship games but this year I have been saying on the sidelines that I'm not going to them this year, I am going to play in it," Armstrong said. "No matter what happens, if we just give our all, we will come out victorious."x
